ParlaMint-AT_2005-04-01-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00101_d7e1835	In the presidential conference, consensus was reached on the design and duration of the debates. Accordingly, a day block time of 9 ‘Vienna hours’ is proposed, so that the following speaking times arise: ÖVP and SPÖ each 158 minutes, Free 108 minutes and Green 117 minutes. The term of office of the member of the government responsible for the respective advisory group, which exceeds 20 minutes, or the speaking time of the Secretary of State responsible for the respective advisory group, which exceeds 10 minutes, shall be taken into account for the speaking time of the respective government group. Furthermore, the speaking time of non-governmental government members or state secretaries will at least be taken into account for the speaking time of the respective government group. With the exception of this, the term of office of the Vice-Chairman shall be used for the general debate held under the Consultative Group I and II, provided that the term of office of the Vice-Chairman is the duration of the debate. It does not exceed 10 minutes, nor does it exceed the speaking time of the Federal Minister for Finance, provided that it does not exceed the duration of 20 minutes. That's what the Hoge Haus decides. We shall now proceed to the vote. I would ask those ladies and gentlemen who agree with this Rules of Procedure to give an appropriate sign. – That is unanimously decided. So we're going to do that. In this context, I can quote a lady who says: “The experiences of young people in school sports are therefore an important step on their way to adult life. Modern sports lessons, well-equipped gyms and many sports additional offers are the conditions for a good promotion of young people in the sports country of Austria. However, they are also the basis for a healthy lifestyle and a meaningful leisure.” This quote, ladies and gentlemen, is from the preface of the Austrian School Sports Report 2004 by the Minister for Education Gehrer. I should like to convey the wishes of the Secretary of State, who also has these facilities for kindergartens. [[State Secretary Mag. Sweitzer: Is there already!]] In view of the problem situation and the above-mentioned confessions, it should be possible to assume that the government has not only recognized the growing movement deficits among children and young people and is taking serious action, but also countering them accordingly. Dear ladies and gentlemen! The reality in schools, however, is another. The range of movements in schools has been greatly reduced by the savings rate in recent years. In many AHS, non-binding exercises in the sports area have simply disappeared. In many schools there were cuts in the movement units. The students are very far away from Schweitzer's daily gymnastics. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) In addition to quality, the quantity of motion offers should also be relevant. Does the school sport offer interest and reach the target group? Is the training of the movement educators in accordance with the new forms of offer? In general, it should be noted that the school is truly not moving much. Hourly sitting in schools and at home in the tasks, and this exercise poverty of children and young people often continues in the private environment. More and more time is spent in front of televisions, computers and other passive entertainment options. All these factors call for their tribute and cause the motor skills of our children to fail. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Sustainable impulses to increase the range of movement in schools are not explicitly included in the education budget or in the sports budget 2006. Rather, there is a tendency to delegate responsibility to parents or to sports clubs. This in conjunction with the current minimum range of movements in schools leaves only one conclusion: that the data on the health and fitness status of children and young people seem to be far too little dramatic for the responsible government members to respond to it. Finally, I should like once again to express my group's wish: Mr Bundeskanzler, Secretary of State, provide for the construction of the Klagenfurt Stadium! - Thank you.

These have been recognized – and for this I thank myself – in recent years, they have become better watchful. We are all involved in further optimizing these framework conditions; together with the representatives of the opposition parties, with Peter Wittmann, with Beate Schasching, with the president of the BSO, ex-minister Lösnak. I think Still that parents are primarily responsible for their children and parents also know best what is good for their children. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Our task, however, is to create the conditions for the optimal development of children. Mr Steier, we are in the process of creating these conditions. Especially in Burgenland, the pilot project “SportKids” is going wonderfully – it is also going wonderfully in the other federal states. However, it is primarily for the sports leaders of the countries and especially for the mayors to implement it on a cross-border basis. We can only develop models on the basis of pilot projects showing that it is possible, but then the countries and communities are on the train. We have jointly, namely the Federal Chancellery with the sports goods industry, the Austrian tourism industry and the Austrian cable cars, for example, reacting to the fact that participation in the ski courses is falling very much. The number of participants has fallen from once 250 000 to 100 000. Together with the above-mentioned organizations, it has succeeded in providing an all-inclusive offer, so that you can travel by 200 € a week on a ski course, get the best material provided, have full board in a wonderful quarter as well as a weekly ski pass, and the transport is included. I think that shows that we are making a great effort to create the appropriate conditions. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We are currently negotiating with the representatives of the Ministry of Education on the design of voluntary afternoon care at schools. A key part of voluntary afternoon care at schools will be sport. We will make it possible for the sports clubs to be completely unbureaucratic and without problems in schools to make their offer to the children. I hope that the children will also make intensive use of it in the course of voluntary afternoon care. I believe that the actions as a whole take account of your intentions, and hope that you will also support us as mayor on site, so that the “SportKids” project, voluntary afternoon care, together with sports in schools, as well as occupational health care, which is part of the “Fit for Austria” project, can be implemented as well as the “Fit for 50 plus” project in your community. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-04-01-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00101_d7e14134	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I should like to address a few errors which unfortunately still prevail in the opposition, in particular with regard to the financing of universities. Unfortunately, Mr Broukal is no longer here, but I have been trying to teach him this for some time. We also try to explain this to him again and again – unfortunately without success. The funding of universities since 2002, since the decision-making of the university law, has to be seen in a completely different light, because there is autonomy there and there is a global budget, partly consisting of individual positions, but with these individual positions the expenditure is reversed. Let us take, for example, the point of officials, the so-called extension of the balance sheet. The fewer officials working at universities, the less expenditure. However, if the global budget remains the same, then the resources available to universities are rising to really use them where they are necessary and where they consider the universities necessary, namely for teaching and research. It must also be stressed again and again, which obviously does not go in or want to go in at the opposition. That's what I'm going to leave open, how you can see it. However, I would also like to address another important aspect. Finally, the question of research activities at universities is also an important point, which since its autonomy, since the universities can also have their resources autonomously, provides for an additional funding rail. After all, it was a very important point in this university reform to create the space for universities, through public-sector research initiatives to manage funding, but also through research tasks, research cooperation with the economy, which will then also help to preserve the university's infrastructure and also create funding for researchers and students involved in research. This has worked really well in recent years; the universities show that they can. One simply cannot let these things fall under the table if one wants to finance science, if one wants to realistically and properly assess the funding of universities. I therefore think that the financial resources provided for in the budget for the coming year are sufficient under these conditions, and that if they are necessary in one area or another – whether for infrastructure or for construction investments – additional funds can be made available as in the past. The most important thing, however, is the flexibility, the opportunity created by autonomy to use financial resources as it is best for the individual university. That was a very, very important step, you must not forget. Therefore, I believe that this funding in the 2006 budget is perfectly sufficient and allows for a good development of universities.

The 2006 budget is the continuation of a successful budgetary policy in this Federal Government. The 2006 budget is aimed at securing and achieving the long-term budget targets. The budget is on four pillars: The balanced budget over the economic cycle by 2008 is one point. This was agreed in the Stability Pact with the countries and communities. In 2000, it was 43.6%, and in 2006 40.6% – that is, 3% less. After all, this is a sum of €7.5 billion, a real discharge! There is investment in the future, for example in the field of research and development. In the years from 2000 to 2006, the figure is at least 10.1 billion, or 34 percent more than in 1995 and 1999. In recent years, investments have also been made in infrastructure, rail and road, which have never been in the history of Austria. Another pillar of the budget is the administrative reform. Since 1999, the Federal Government alone has had a savings of 1.4 billion schillings. For research, I would like to say the following: The research block has a plus of 2.2 million or almost 2.6 percent, totaling 90.2 million. ParlaMint-AT_2005-04-01-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00101_d7e17051	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! I would like to say briefly to my budget that, like any budget, the judicial budget is, of course, a compromise. I mean, that's what we're quite clear about. Each budget is negotiated, and of course we too are from the judicial centre with higher Ideas entered into the budget negotiations; like any other department. But we have achieved very good results in our budgetary negotiations. In the Federal Estimate 2004 total expenditure was €874.9 million, and in the Federal Estimate 2005 the figure was €975.9 million, and the same number is also available in 2006. You will see that in 2005 we had an increase in our budget of 11 percent compared to the 2004 federal estimate. If I now calculate this for two years, this means at least an increase of 5.5 per cent for each year, and I must say that this has been a very good negotiation result. I would also like to stress at this point that as Minister of Justice, I am very proud that we have the highest degree of ownership of all departments in the Federal Government; this too deserves to be mentioned. We have an own coverage of 74.5 percent. This means that in the budget we now have, the Republic of Austria, our citizens are actually being burdened with an amount of only around € 248 million. This amount is practically used for the execution of the sentence, where I also understand that this is a state task and the responsibility for it is clearly with the state. There has also been an increase in crime and, above all, the increasing numbers of prisoners. I have already reported on this subject several times, but would like to stress once again that this is a Central European development. Austria is not alone with rising numbers of prisoners. I have a lot of contact with colleagues from the EU, but also with colleagues from the West Balkans, for example, and it is generally clear that we have increasing numbers of prisoners in Central Europe. The reasons for this can also be clearly seen from our arrest statistics. If we had a share of 29 percent of foreign crime in 2000 and are now 44 percent of some 9 000 foreigners, this is a clear language. Unfortunately, we are here in the east of the EU, so we have a Schengen external border, but I am sure that over the next few years – probably not yet – these figures will certainly improve due to an increased fight against organised crime and also increased cooperation in this area. I was also referred to whether there are alternatives to these high arrests. Of course! There are alternatives, and we are also considering applying these alternatives. The model of conditional dismissal was also addressed several times. In this connection, however, I should like to point out to the House – I am sure that each one of you knows this anyway – that it is a question of jurisdiction. Whether or not the Human beings are released or not, is a decision of jurisdiction and thus completely removed from my sphere of influence. There is also a model with regard to electronic foot shackles. We will certainly continue to work on this. There is another model with non-profit work instead of replacement sentences. There will be a pilot project in autumn, and I hope it will succeed in finding a solution. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Another solution, which I also call for – and this at European level – is the execution of sanctions in the home country.
